[trialAccount t18210912-170]
1219. THOMAS MORAND was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of September, four seals, value 39 s., the goods of Francis West.
FRANCIS WEST. I am an optician and sell seals, and live in Russell-court, Drury-lane. On the 12th of September, about seven o'clock, the prisoner came in and asked to see some gold seals which were in the window, I shewed him a card, containing five; he appeared to fix on one marked 7 s. 6 d., and asked me the motto; I told him - he then said, his brother liked a black stone, but he liked a white; I asked if he intended to purchase, he said, Yes, and asked me to cut the seal off; I said I could not, unless he meant to purchase; he said he did - I cut it off, he then came very close to the lamp. He had his hat on, which was wet; I told him, if he went near the glass bell over the lamp it would fly to pieces; he took it off, and began talking about his brother Tom - he went to the door, called "Tom," and ran out with the card of four seals. I followed immediately and knocked him down, I secured him without loosing sight of him. I suppose he dropped them in the mud, as I have not found them. I distinctly saw him take them. On the way to Bow-street I met Dallas, who took him back to my house to search him, and there he exchanged my coat for his, which I did not discover till I got to Bow-street.
ALEXANDER DALLAS. I met him in Vinegar-yard. I stripped him in the parlour.
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Seven Years.
Second Middlesex Jury, before W. Arabin, Esq.
[trialAccount t18210912-171]
1120. JAMES FLANERY and THOMAS WASHER were indicted for feloniously breaking and entering the dwelling-house of Ebenezer Norman, at St. George, about six o'clock in the forenoon on the 8th of August, (he and others being therein), and stealing three spoons, value 10 s., and four crown pieces, and eightpence in copper money, his property.
EBENEZER NORMAN. I am a cabinet-maker, and rent a house in Little Turner-street, Commercial-road, St. George's in the East. On the 8th of August, between five and six o'clock in the morning, I and my family were all in bed. I was awoke by a loud knocking at the street door, saying, "Norman, get up, there are thieves in your wood yard". I immediately came down, went to the private door to open it, seeing the chain down, I could not open it, which I thought strange, as the bolts were undone - one of the witnesses was holding it to prevent their escaping - I then ran into the shop, opened the shop door, and found my neighbours in front - I instantly ran down the wood yard, and after looking about some time, saw Washer on the top of the adjoining house; I kept my eye on him - they got a neighbour's ladder, put it against the wall, and I saw the witness collar one of them; I went and slipped on my things, went round to the street, and helped to take them to the watch-house - both prisoners were found on the top of the house. I lost a table-spoon and two tea-spoons, four crown pieces, and some halfpence; I had left them in the back parlour cupboard.
WILLIAM DEAN. On the 8th of August, early in the morning, I heard a noise in the street, got up, ran to the the window, and saw Norman in his shirt, in the street; I ran down stairs, and understood that thieves were backward - I kept outside the door, and presently saw a person's hat on the top of the house, above the coping; I watched till somebody brought a ladder - I then got on the top of the house, and found both the prisoners lying in the gutter; I collared Washer, and said, "Come, get up," and in rising, some silver spoons dropped from under his smock frock, and a brown paper with some copper in it. I handed him down the ladder to the watchman, then went back, Flanery still laid in the gutter; I handed him down the ladder to the watchman, we all went to the watch-house, and there two crown pieces were found in one of their bats.
ISAAC CALWAY. I got up about five o'clock in the morning, and went into the Commercial-road; as I returned, I saw Flanery in the passage of Norman's house, and Washer leaning with his back against the gate outside - as I passed by, they came out of the passage and went towards the Commercial-road - by that time, I had got home, which is thirty yards off. I opened my window, looked out, and saw them both return to the same spot; it struck me all was not right, I went up by Norman's house, and met a man at the top of the road, and told him I thought all was not right; I went up to Norman's door, gave a good knock, and directly heard something inside - I looked over the yard door, and Flanery was on the planks in the yard coming towards me, he made his way back again, and they got on the adjoining house - I saw them in the gutter.
FRANCIS JACKSON. I am watch-house keeper. About half-past six o'clock I was called up to take the prisoners, I found two crown pieces in Washer's hat, and on Flanery I found a few coppers, the other property was given to me.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoners made no defence.
FLANERY - GUILTY. - DEATH. Aged 17.
WASHER - GUILTY. - DEATH. Aged 16.
Second Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18210912-172]
1221. JAMES MARONEY and THOMAS FINNERTY were indicted for stealing, on the 7th of August, two scarfs, value 16 s.; one apron, value 1 s.; one handkerchief, value1 s.; one ball of cotton, value 1 s., and one brush, value 1 s., the goods of Jane Elcom.
JANE ELCOM. On the 6th of August I was stopping at a friend's, who keeps the Hare and Hounds, public-house, Baltick-street. About half-past eight o'clock in the morning, the room door was broken open, and my box containing this property taken out; the box was brought home empty that night, I suspected the prisoners. They did not belong to the house.
JOHN BARTLETT. I was sent for, and went to a house in Ivy-street, St. Giles's, and found these things carelessly thrown down. I took Mary Maloney and Lyons in custody, they were admitted as evidence. I then took Maroney, he told me that he planned to rob the house, that he and three more boys took the box to Finnerty's lodgings. Mrs. Maloney lives in the parlour where I found the things. Finnerty was taken a few days after.
JOHN DAVIS. On the morning of the robbery, I accompanied Bartlett to the house and found the property.
MARY MALONEY. I live in Ivy-street, St. Giles's, near George-street, I live in the cellar. Lyon's called me to give her a light in the passage, I came up, there was a barrow in the passage, and a blue box between the handles of it; whether Maroney or Lyons took it into Lyons's room, I cannot say. The box was opened, and Maroney took the things out, tied them up, and took them away; he said, he got them from the Hare and Hounds, public-house. I told Lyons they were stolen, and it was a shame, and told the neighbours. She took the box to Elcom's after it was emptied - they took all the things out, and took them away. Finnerty was in the passage, but not in the room; next morning, the articles stated in the indictment were found in the passage where the barrow stood.
LEWIS ROBERTS. I apprehended Finnerty between twelve and one o'clock at night, on the 9th.
FINNERTY'S Defence. Mrs. Maloney said, she would have nothing to do with it, but she got half a pint of gin for it.
MARONEY. - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
FINNERTY. - NOT GUILTY.
Second Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18210912-173]
1222. MARY JACKSON was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of September, 10 s. 11 d., in monies numbered, the property of Job Mulcock, from his person.
JOB MULCOCK. On the 2d of September, about two o'clock in the morning, I picked the prisoner up by Westminster-abbey; she asked me to go home with her, I said, I had no objection. We went to No. 8, Pye-street, on the first floor. I staid there about three hours; she called me - I said I was not going to get up - she left the room. I awoke afterwards, and missed my money from my coat pocket - I gave her 2 s. - I was sober - I live at Knightsbridge. I found I was locked in the room, and could not get out for near an hour, when a girl let me out. I told the landlord of it - he made very light of it. I stopped there till eight o'clock. When I was going out, she came up the street as drunk as possible. I told her I should get a constable - I fetched one, and we met her coming out of the house. I am sure she is the woman, nobody but her was in the room.
BENJAMIN TIMBRELL. Between seven and eight o'clock on Sunday, the prosecutor fetched me to Pye-street. I saw the prisoner coming out of the house; he said she was the woman. I took her, and found one shilling and some halfpence on her; he said he could swear to one penny piece among them.
JOB MULCOCK. I know this penny-piece was in my pocket.
Prisoner's Defence. I took the penny-piece of Mr. Thompson, of Charing-cross.
NOT GUILTY.
Second Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18210912-174]
1223. THOMAS JOHNSON was indicted for stealing on the 4th of August, one copper, value 18 s., the goods of Samuel Shelly.
JOHN SMITH. I am foreman to Mr. Shelly. This copper stood at his door - I know it to be his.
JOHN BARNARD. I was left in care of the shop, and saw the prisoner two doors off with it on his back, I stopped him; he said a man gave him sixpence to carry it.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was employed by another person to take it to Great Queen-street, and he would give me sixpence. I did not consider what I was doing.

1224. HENRY JOHNSON was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of August, one shirt, value 3 s.; one coat, value 2 s., and one handkerchief, value 6 d., the goods of John Kelchea.
JOHN KELCHEA. I am a labourer. I was looking for work, and went to a public-house in Charles-street, St. Giles's, to ask for a lodging; the prisoner came up and said he knew where I could get one at an honest place, he took me to Church-street, St. Giles's - the woman of the house was out. I thought I would shave myself, put my bundle on the table while I shaved, and he went out with my shirt, coat, and handkerchief. He was stopped and the duplicates found on him.
Prisoner. Q. Did you see me take them out - A. I missed them the moment he was gone.
HENRY WILLIAM POWELL. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Bridge-road, Lambeth. On the 11th of August, the prisoner pawned a shirt for 3 s. - my duplicate was found on him.
PHILIP RILEY. On the 11th of August, I took him in charge, and found three shillings and the duplicate on him of a waistcoat, shirt, and jacket, all pawned that day.
(Shirt produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Transported for Seven Years.
Second Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

1225. JOHN ISAACS was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of August, 9 lbs. of lead, value 18 d., and one sheet, value 2 s., the goods of Robert Burdett.
ROBERT BURDETT. I am a weaver, and live in Phoenix-street; this lead was in my shop, I use it in my business. On the 22d of August, I was in bed - my wife awoke meI looked out of the window, and saw a man, who asked, if I had authorised any one to take the lead from my house. I went and found it at the watch-house, returned and missed it. I know nothing of the prisoner.
FRANCES BURDETT. I am wife of last witness. I went up and missed a sheet off the bed of the top room.
ROBERT WILKINSON. I am apprentice to Mr. Burdett. Isaacs came up when I was in bed at my masters - he sent a girl up to call me. I got up and came down; he asked if I could give him a lodging; I said "No;" he said, if I did not, he should be put in the watch-house or something. I agreed to it, and in the morning he got up an hour before me, and awoke me about half-past five o'clock; he asked if I would run away from my uncle; I said, No, I did not want to go. I went down stairs on purpose to get him down; he asked if he might put on my shoes. I said Yes; as they were an old pair, and about four doors from the house he tried the shoes on, and said he could not wear them. He went up again, and brought the sheet down, he said, "Now go away with me." I did not like to go back, and we came away together, and when we got out of the street, he said, he had some lead, and asked me to carry it as it was all falling down in his breeches, and I did.
Prisoner. Q. Did you not ask me to fetch you a wh - e - A. No; I did not persuade him to run away with me.
FRANCIS MANNOCK. I was officer of the night. About half-past five o'clock in the morning, I left the watch-house to go home, and saw the prisoner, who appeared a suspicious character, with something like a sheet stuck out of his coat, which was buttoned. I asked what he had there? he said it was nothing to me. I said he should not pass till I was satisfied; he then said it was a sheet which he was taking from his mother to his aunt. He could not tell where his mother or aunt lived - I took him and Wilkinson to the watch-house. I found a sheet round the prisoner's body, in his hat a shirt, and in his pocket a dirty cloth - he said the sheet was his aunt's. On Wilkinson, I found 8 lbs. of lead in one pocket, and 1 lb. in the other; he said the prisoner stole it from his master, and after he left gave it him to carry, as he had made up his mind to leave his master.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. This lad's uncle came to him at the watch-house and said, "What Bob, you have robbed me six or seven times before, and I shall go to the utmost of the law, and what have you led this young man into it for."
ROBERT BURDETT. I never said any such thing.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.
Second Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18210912-177]
1226. JOSEPH HUDSON, was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of September, 2 s. 1 1/2 d., the property of Anthony Starkey.
ANTHONY STARKEY. I am a fishmonger, and live at Hackney. On the 12th or 13th of September, I marked my money and put it on my desk in the parlour. The prisoner has lived five or six years with me, I suspected him, as he dressed a good deal - I marked the shillings and halfpence in the evening, and next morning 1 s. 7 d. was gone. I sent for an officer, who searched him - he gave me a key of his box from his pocket, which I tried to my desk, it fitted it perfectly well, he then gave me some silver from his waistcoat pocket, 2 s. of which I knew, and in his coat pocket three halfpence were found, which I had marked - he had no occasion to go to the desk.
JOHN GARVA. I am beadle of Hackney. I was fetched and took the prisoner in custody - found two shillings and three halfpence in his pocket, and a key which opened the desk - he said it was the key of his box, and that he had no money about him.
MR. STARKEY. The money is mine.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Judgment Respited.
Second Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

1227. STEPHEN HUNTLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of August, three tea-spoons, value 10 s., and one table-spoon, value 8 s., the goods of Richard Battley .
RICHARD BATTLEY. I am a commercial agent, and live at Clapton. On the 3d of August, about half-past three o'clock in the afternoon, I was sitting in my back parlour, and saw the prisoner in my back garden, he went into the kitchen and took three tea and one table spoon from the drawer; when he came up, I asked his business; he asked if I wanted any shavings for my stove - I said, "Yes," and he immediately ran away - I followed and caught him, he gave me a tea-spoon, and said, that was all he had taken, and hoped I would forgive him. On searching further, I found the other spoons in his basket among the shavings.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 17.

1228. HENRY HANDLEY and MARY NICHOLLS was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of July, one bed, value 10 s.; three curtains, value 3 s.; one iron, value 6 d.; one bolster, value 2 s.; two pillow-cases, value 2 s.; two blankets, value 5 s.; one quilt, value 4 s., and one sheet, value 3 s., the goods of Jane Tewart, in a lodging-room.
JANE TEWART. I am a widow, and live at Ratcliff-highway. Handley came and took a lodging - I wished to see his wife, and in the evening he brought this woman - I let them these things, with my second floor, at 4 s. a week - they stopped for five weeks and paid me for three; my daughter went into the room and missed some things - I went in with the officer and missed all these things, I think the woman did it.
DANIEL KENNEDY. I am a pawnbroker. All the articles stated in the indictment were pawned at my house, except the feathers of the bed, the tick was pawned - the female prisoner pawned them all.
GEORGE MURRANT. I am a Thames police officer. I searched Nicholls' house and found the duplicates on the mantle-piece - they are all pawned in the man's name; she said she sold the feathers, but would not tell me where.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
HANDLEY'S Defence. I was absent at the time.
NICHOLL'S Defence. I took them, being in distress - he knew nothing of it.
HANDLEY - GUILTY. Aged 39.
Confined Two Months.
NICHOLLS - GUILTY. Aged 36.
Confined Three Months.
Second Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

1229. JOHN HARWOOD was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of September, one toast rack, value 7 s., the goods of James Shabot.
ROBERT LONG. I am servant to Mr. James Shabot, a merchant, of Montague-street, Russell-square. On the 8th of September, about ten o'clock in the morning, I was at breakfast, the cook came down from the dining-room, and said I had not put the heater in the fire; I went into the pantry and saw the prisoner put his hand through the iron bar, the window was open, and take the toast rack - he had come down the area steps. I ran after him - he threw it down in Montague-street - I caught him in Bury-street, and gave him in charge - I never lost sight of him.
WILLIAM WALTON. I was in the mews and saw the prisoner run across with the toast rack, he threw it down at my feet, I picked it up - Long was following and took him.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I heard the cry and ran into Bury-street after the thief.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Seven Years.
Second Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

1230. MARY GRIFFITHS was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of August, one candlestick, value 2 s., the goods of William Hitchcock.
WILLIAM HITCHCOCK. I am a publican, and live in Old-street. On the 14th of August, about half-past six o'clock at night, two gentlemen came into my parlour to write, and had a candlestick on the table. I went out, and did not miss it till the next morning; the prisoner was frequently at my house, selling buttons.
SAMUEL BRADY. I am a publican, and live near Hitchcock. The prisoner came into my house on this night, about half-past ten o'clock, she said something, and two Scotch gentlemen said, "Turn that woman out;" I went to turn her out, and saw the candlestick sticking out of her pocket, I took it from her, she said it was her own; I sent for the watchman, who took her.
JOHN TWEEDY. I am a constable. On the 14th of August, I took charge of the prisoner, and asked her what had become of the candlestick, she said she never saw it before.
JOSEPH PRINCE. I was taking the prisoner to the watch-house, she said if I would forgive her, she would shew me where she took it from - I took her to several houses, and when we came to the Rum Puncheon public-house, where Hitchcock lives, she said "That is it."
(Property produced and sworn to.)
(The prisoner put in a written defence, stating, that she had drank rather freely, and did not know she had the candlestick. till it was found on her.)
GUILTY. Aged 63.
Confined Three Months.
Second Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

JAMES METCALF. I am a constable. On the 22d of August, about eight o'clock at night, I was in St. John-street, opposite Mr. Garland's, and saw Green and Dougan come up to this handkerchief, the prisoners were all three together, a few yards from the shop; one of them cut or tore down some handkerchiefs, which hung at the door - Haylett stood five or six yards off, the other two beckoned to her, she went up and took the handkerchief, and crossed the street, saw me, and set off running - I went and took them out of her apron, the other two were taken.

1234. MATILDA DOWD was indicted, for that she, on the 19th of August, a certain female child, called Louisa Smith, daughter of Frederick William Smith, and the late Eliza his wife, of the age of five years, maliciously and feloniously did decoy and entice away, with intent to deprive the said Frederick William Smith of the possession of her, by concealing and detaining her from him.
SECOND COUNT, stating her intent to be to steal the articles of apparel and other things worn and about the person of the said child.
FREDERICK WILLIAM SMITH. I live in Queen-street, Percival-street, Clerkenwell, and have a daughter named Louisa, she is five years old. On Sunday the 19th of August, about half-past seven o'clock in the evening, I was informed the child had been stolen, and was on its way to my house; I ran out and found the people in King-street, bringing the prisoner back with it. I had been at the parlour window all the afternoon, and saw the child at the door at six o'clock.
ANN FOSKETT. I live next door to Mr. Smith. On Sunday evening about half-past six or nearer seven o'clock, I saw the prisoner at Smith's door, the child was in the parlour, she stood at the railing, and beckoned her through the parlour window, I went in doors returned in two minutes, and saw her take hold of the child's hand, and walk down Queen-street with it.
GEORGE HART. I am a blind painter, and live in Tottenham-court-road. On Sunday the 19th of August, a little after seven o'clock, I was in Goswell-street, about a quarter of a mile from Queen-street, and saw the prisoner walking towards Old-street with this child in her arms, she going away from Queen-street, the child was repeatedly calling to be carried home and seemed uneasy. I requested her to take it home, she said she was going home; I asked where she lived, she said in Holborn. I then suspected that she did not know the way, or that it was not her child, and asked whose child it was, she said it was her own. The child kept crying to be taken home, I asked if she knew the way she was going, and told her she was not going in a direction for Holborn, and asked her what part of Holborn she lived in; she said in Hatton-garden. I asked her what number, she would not answer. I then told her the child was stolen, and I would accompany her to the watch-house or workhouse. She remained where she was, a crowd collected round, and a little girl said she had seen the child in Queen-street, we took her back to Queen-street, and on the road she said she had just come from Chelsea. The child is now in Court.
Prisoner. Q. Did I say the child was mine - A. Yes.
SAMUEL GRIBBLE. I am a constable. The prisoner was given in my charge, she said she did not mean to hurt the child. I asked her where she got it from, she said from the the corner of Red Lion-street, that she lived in Eyre-street, Hatton-garden, and had left her husband at Chelsea with three children. Several people followed us, and there was a cry of "Rescue" several times.
Prisoner's Defence. I met a man whom I had not seen for two or three years, he took me to Oxford-street. I met this child child crying, and two or three people round it, it wanted to go home, I asked where it lived, it could not tell. I took it down two or three streets to see if any one owned it.
JUROR to SMITH. Q. Can the child talk and say where it lives - A. Yes, Sir.
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Transported for Seven Years.
First Middlesex Jury, before W. Arabin, Esq.
[trialAccount t18210912-186]
1235. GEORGE CUFFLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, one table spoon, value 10 s., and one pair of shoes, value 4 s., the goods of John Conner.
JOHN CONNER. I am a dyer, and live in Wheeler-street. The prisoner was in my service as a labourer; these spoons were in my lapidary. I lost them on the 23d or 24th of August, and on the 25th Kennedy produced them. I lost a pair of shoes from the same place.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. He lived with you - A. Yes. I always found him honest. I missed the things, but do not know who took them.
GEORGE GRANT. I am a weaver, and live in Phoenix-street. On the 23d of August I was talking to two friends about having some porter, the prisoner came up, he pulled a table spoon out of his pocket, and said he had a spoon, I said it was too late in the evening to have a spoon to get something to drink with, he left us, we went into the public-house, he came in after us and offered me the spoon if I would pay for a pot of beer for him. I kept it till I came out, and then seeing a crest on it, I gave it to Kennedy.
JAMES KENNEDY. I have the spoon. I have known the prisoner from an infant, and never knew him in custody. I found the shoes at his lodging, he told me they were there.
GUILTY. Aged 33.
Confined One Year.
First Middlesex Jury, before W. Arabin, Esq.

1236. GEORGE CLAY was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of August, seven yards of linen, value 10 s., and one waistcoat piece, value 3 s., the goods of John Denford.
ROBERT HEAKES. I am servant to Mr. John Denford, haberdasher, Little Newport-street, Soho. On the 7th of August, about one o'clock the prisoner came to the shop, and looked at some Irish linen for shirts; he asked how much would make two shirts; I cut him off seven yards and three-quarters of yard of cord for a waistcoat, he then said he had not money enough in his pocket, and if I could send our lad with him, he was going to Covent-garden to meet his father, and would return the money by the lad. I sent the boy with him, and the goods, with orders to bring back the money. I never intended to part with them without it.
JOSEPH HEAKES. I am fourteen years old. I was sent with the prisoner, I carried the parcel and was not to part with it without the money, he took me the Coach-maker's Arms, public-house, at the corner of Hanover-street, Long-acre. He said his father belonged to the letter carrier, and he expected to meet him there. After waiting three-quarters of an hour, he said he supposed his father had stoped at a public-house in Drury-lane, we went and waited there about an hour; he said as his father was notcome he would go to his sister's, and get the money, and told me to be sure and be particular in taking care of these parcels of his, particularly the small one, as there was something very valuable in it, and he would take my parcel with him as it would save him the trouble of going back again; he went with the parcel, and after waiting a considerable time I came home with the parcels, and on opening them found they were filled with grass. I never saw him till he was taken.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Did he say how long you were to wait - A. No. He had ale and porter and pressed me to drink. He asked me for the bundle and I gave it him, as he said something valuable was in his. I expected him to return. I did not ask where his sister lived.
RICHARD BIRCH. I am a pawnbroker. The property was pawned with me on the 9th of August I do not know the man.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Confined One Year.
First Middlesex Jury, before W. Arabin, Esq.

1237. WILLIAM BRIANT was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of August, one seal, value 1 l.; two keys, value 10 s., and part of a watch chain, value 5 s., the goods of Dennis Francis Humbert, from his person.
DENNIS FRANCIS HUMBERT. I am a coal-merchant. On the 26th of August, about a quarter past nine o'clock at night, I was at the corner of Neptune-street, Ratcliffe-highway, walking with a lady, my daughter and another lady were behind, and behind them my son. Just as I got round the corner into Wellclose-square, I saw three boys apparently at play, one (the prisoner) ran between me and the wall, stooped down so as to get under my arm, and snatched at my seals, the chain broke, he got part of it with the keys and seal, I immediately turned round and called "Stop thief, I am robbed;" he nearly knocked my daughter down. My son hearing the cry ran after him, and one of the three came before him, but he was taken without my losing sight of him, the others got away.
- HUMBERT. My father's account is correct, the prisoner is the lad.
Prisoner's Defence. I was walking round the square, heard the cry, ran up, and they laid hold of me.
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Seven Years.
First Middlesex Jury, before W. Arabin, Esq.

1239. WILLIAM DEAN was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of July, 39 lbs. of lead, value 3 s., the goods of Michael M'Donough, and fixed to his dwelling house.
SECOND COUNT, stating it to be fixed to a building of his.
ROBERT CLAYARDS. I live in Crown-street, St. Giles's, next door but one to Mr. M'Donough. I keep pigeons on my roof. On the 28th of July, about nine o'clock in the morning, I was on the roof attending to them, and saw the prisoner on M'Donough's roof (he lodged next door), in the act of cutting the lead from the loft door; I walked across the next house, and stood by the chimney; he cut it away, brought it to the gutter, and was in the act of cutting it into smaller pieces when he saw me. I said. "You have no right to this, I will take it." I took it down into my own house and gave it to the officer, and told M'Donough, he sent for an officer who took him. I let him go down through his own house.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Were you on the roof before him - A. No, he was just beginning when I went up; he is a bricklayer, he came to me before the officer came, and said he was sorry for what he had done; the officer came in half an hour, he remained at home.
MICHAEL M'DONOUGH. I live next door but one to Clayard's, and am a carver and gilder. I sent Lewis to match the lead on the trap door - it fitted.
Cross-examined. Q. Are you a housekeeper - A. Yes; it is in St. Giles's parish. I find the prisoner was an industrious man, has a large family, and was in distress,
LEWIS LEWIS. I fitted the lead to the roof.
GUILTY. Aged 43.
Confined Six Months.
First Middlesex Jury, before W. Arabin, Esq.

1242. PHOEBE BENJAMIN was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of August, one watch, value 15 s.; one seal, value 3 s., and one key, value 2 s., the goods of George Buckley from his person.
GEORGE BUCKLEY. I am steward of an East Indiaman. On the 15th of August at half-past two o'clock in the morning, I was in Whitechapel, going home to Kennigton-lane - I had been drinking, but knew what I was about, and met the prisoner. I am sure she is the woman; we chatted together for three minutes, she asked me to give her something to drink, I gave her 6 d., my brother, mother, and sister, were about four yards a-head, agreeing for a hackney coach, she kept me in conversation three minutes, I then left her, she was close to me, when I gave her the money, a man said feel if you have got your watch, I felt and missed it; it was safe just before - we went after her with the watchman, who overtook her - she swore she had not got it, he overhawled her, and found it in her bosom.
SAMUEL STOCKTON. I am a watchman. Buckley came to me; I overtook the prisoner and asked if she had got the watch, she said she had not. I found it in her bosom.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. He gave it me to keep till morning, I walked off with it.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Life.
First Middlesex Jury, before W. Arabin, Esq.
[trialAccount t18210912-194]
1243. JOHN BLAZER was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, one shawl, value 35 s., the goods of George Sparing.
CAROLINE SPARING. I lodge in Pear-street, Westminster. On the 20th of August, I met the prisoner in Queen-street; I knew him by sight before, I asked him to drink, as I knew his brother; I never saw him before, but by his features, I asked if he was not that man's brother, he said, Yes; we went to the Cooper's-arms, public-house, and had two pots of beer - it was about half-past ten o'clock at night. I was taken very poorly there, and a friend of mine who was there took me home, the prisoner went with me, they both stripped me, and put me to bed, he sat on the chair by the bed, I asked him to go home, he pretended to be in liquor; he took and folded my shawl up, I asked what he was doing with it; he then laid it on the table, I went to sleep, and felt him taking my pocket from under my head; I said, "Young man, do not take my things;" and he went out with my shawl, leaving my pocket on the table, and no money in it, there was a candle in the room all the time.
Q. Had you sent for any doctor - A. No; I am married, my husband's name is George, he has left me - I have not found the shawl, I saw him next morning, and asked him for it, he pushed me down, and went off.
Prisoner. Q. Were you not in liquor - A. No; not at all, I had not been drinking all day with him.
COURT. Q. Do you mean to say he did not meet you before half-past ten o'clock - A. I believe it was between six and seven o'clock, I met him standing at the door of the Hoop and Grapes, public-house, he treated me with a pot of beer there, we had nothing but three pots of beer.
ANN BRANSTON. I was present. The prosecutrix has spoken a number of untruths.
NOT GUILTY.
First Middlesex Jury, before W. Arabin, Esq.
[trialAccount t18210912-195]
1244. MARGARET BARLOW was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of September, one watch, value 2 l.; one seal, value 1 l.; one key, value 2 d.; one ribbon, value 1 d.; one pocket-book, value 6 d., and one rule, value 6 d., the goods of William Harrison, from his person.
WILLIAM HARRISON. I am a journeyman stone-mason, and live in Tooley-street. On the 13th of September, about two o'clock in the morning, I met the prisoner in the Strand, we went to several public-houses together, and about four o'clock, I accompanied her to No. 22, Clement's-lane. I put a washing tub against the door, and laid on the bed with my trowsers on, and put my watch in my right hand breeches pocket, I gave her what silver I had. My pocket-book, containing 2 l., which was in my coat pocket, was across a chair. I awoke about eight o'clock in the morning, and found her gone, and missed my watch, put my coat on, and found I was fastened in the room - I made a noise at the door, and old man came up, and I asked him to let me out, he said he could not. Sometime after, the prisoner came and let me out; I charged her with robbing me, she denied it, and said, I never had a watch - I got an officer (after going to several places) who searched the room, and found my pocket and rule on the table, they were not there when I charged her with stealing my watch - nothing was found on her, the watch has been found - I had been at the Play till eleven o'clock, then went to a public-house or two, I might have drank two pots of beer before I met her - I was not intoxicated.
JOHN LANGFIELD. I was sent for between nine and ten o'clock in the morning. The prosecutor was sober then, he charged the prisoner about the watch, she denied it, I found the pocket-book and rule on the table, and found the watch on Crampton.
JAMES CRAMPTON. I am a porter, and live in the same house as the prisoner, the landlady gave me the duplicate of the watch, and I redeemed it.
MARGARET MOORE. The prisoner lodges at my brother's. I picked the duplicate off the kitchen floor; I do not know who it came from.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
(The prisoner in the defence stated that she went toseveral houses with the prosecutor, and in the morning he sent her to pawn the watch to get a breakfast.)
NOT GUILTY.
First Middlesex Jury, before W. Arabin, Esq.
[trialAccount t18210912-196]
THIRTEENTH DAY,
WEDNESDAY, SEPTEMBER 26.
1245. JOSEPH WILKS was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of August, one Bath stove, value 7 s., the goods of Leonard Macknelly.
WILLIAM KELLY. I live with Leonard Macknelly, who is a broker, in Long-alley, Worship-street. On the 27th of August, I was up stairs looking out of window, and saw a man looking at a Bath stove which stood at the door; my mistress went to the door, the man was gone then, and there was a mob round the stove.
Q. Did you see who took it - A. No; I was examined before the Magistrate, and told the truth there - I do not know who the man was, he went away after I missed the stove, he was looking at it, and when mistress went to the door, he was gone - I found the stove up the court by a house, it was not covered over.
Q. How old are you - A. I am going on for fifteen. There was nothing on the stove, I never said that there was.
Q. Did you run down to your mistress, and tell her the stove was gone - A. No; I did not tell the Magistrate where it was taken; I do not know who dropped it; I did not tell the Magistrate who did. Nothing was read over to me before the Magistrate.
Q. I caution you, you are fifteen years old, and old enough to know what you are about; you must tell the truth, and nothing but the truth; I want to know if you ever said at any place, or before the Magistrate that you saw a man take the stove - A. No; I never did - I did not go after any body or overtake any body, I saw nobody drop it, I did not run after the prisoner; I never told the Magistrate that the prisoner was the man who took it, and that I saw him take it.
Q. Mind what you are about, I caution you again to tell the truth; you will be committed and tried unless you do; I ask you whether you did not swear before the Magistrate that you saw the prisoner at the bar take the stove, go away with it, that you went after him, overtook him, and asked him to bring it back - A. No; I never said it nor swore it.
Q. Who was taken in custody that day - A. I do not know rightly who was the man, I saw nobody taken in custody - nobody has been talking to me about this prosecution - I recollect going before the Magistrate at Worship-street, I was examined, but did not give evidence against the man; what I said was taken down in writing, it was not read over to me.
Q. Now boy, let me caution you to speak the truth, look at this (the deposition) do you remember putting your mark to it - A. Not as I remember.
Q. Now the officer is here who heard the paper read to you, saw you put your mark to it, and while it was being written down, heard the statement which is put on that paper come from your lips; do you mean to say after that, that it was never read over to you, and that you did not state and relate what is written in that paper - (No answer).
Q. Do you not know you put your mark to it - A. Not that I know of.
JURY. Q. Had you a pen in your hand - A. Not that I know of.
COURT. Q. Who has convered with you about this business - A. Nobody; the prisoner's friends nor my master never spoke to me about it - I did not see the prisoner nor any body take the stove, nor ever said that I did.
Cross-examined by MR. ANDREWS. Q. You were at the two pair of stairs window when you saw this going on - A. Yes.
HANNAH MACKNELLY. I am the wife of Leonard Macknelly. On Monday the 27th of August, between twelve and one o'clock, as near as I can recollect; a little boy in the neighbourhood, named Frank Groom, told me the stove was taken away; it stood outside the door. I went out, the prisoner was pointed out to me. The stove laid on the ground.
Q. How far did you go before you got up to him - A. He turned the corner towards Finsbury-market. I think I went about twenty yards.
Q. Did you see a man drop the stove - A. It was on the ground when I came up. I did not see him drop it, he stood close to it. I was sworn before the Magistrate, and what I said was taken down. I put my mark to it. I spoke the truth then the same as I do now.
Q. You did not see him drop it - A. He was stooping over it just as if he was laying it down; it was too heavy for him, he being in liquor. I brought him and the boy brought the stove back.
Q. What boy - A. Kelly. He got up before me.
Q. Did you see him when the man put the stove down - A. He was with the man before I got to him, he saw more than I did.
Q. You say the prisoner was drunk - A. Very drunk indeed, I do not think he could carry the stove further than the twenty yards. I had seen the stove at the door an hour before.
Q. Did you say before the Magistrate
"I ran round the corner and saw the prisoner drop the stove now produced" - A. Yes, I did say it.
Q. Then why not tell us the truth here - A. I am sorry to see a man of those years -
Cross-examined. Q. What do you mean by his dropping it - A. He had it in his arms - he laid it on the ground. The first information I had was from a strange boy. I saw a man named Hawkes there - he is a man I would not trust. I said at the time that the prisoner would not have been there, if Hawkes had not brought him. I sent for a constable - the prisoner went away and came back again; the stove is not worth much - there was more valuable things, and a good deal better stoves. I have not seen Hawkes since.
COURT. Q. Am I to understand you saw him place the stove out of his hand on the ground - A. When I pushed the mob away it was on the ground.
Q. You saw him place it out of his hand on the ground - A. Yes.
PATRICK JAMES MASON. On the 27th of August, I was sent for from Worship-street, this boy and another came; the prisoner was walking by - the boy pointed him out, and said, "That is the man who stole the stove" - the prisoner appeared fresh. The parties were examined before the Magistrate the same day at two o'clock. I heard the boy examined, what he said was taken down and afterwards read over to him, and I saw him put his mark to it; I heard the woman examined, what she said was taken down - it was read over to her and she put her mark to it; I heard her state what she signed.
Cross-examined. Q. What office do you belong to - A. I belonged to Bow-street. I have given it up to Barnard Gleed.
Q. On your oath were you not dismissed for misconduct - A. I was not. I was discharged for being drunk on duty. I am now a willow cutter. I was an officer at this time.
Prisoner's Defence. I was coming by and am entirely innocent of knowing any thing was stolen. I had been drinking with a shopmate.
BARNARD GLEED. I am an officer, and have been so twenty years. Mason brought the prisoner and stove to the office - the Magistrate desired me to take care of it. I heard the boy examined - what he said was taken down by the clerk; it was read over to him, and he made his mark. What the woman said was also taken down - she made her mark to it. The prisoner certainly was extremely drunk.
Cross-examined. Q. Was he so drunk as to be insensible - A. I think he would not have done it if he had been sober.
Q. Were there not so many people about, that the boy might be told by somebody that the prisoner was the man - A. He might.
- GIBBONS. I saw the prisoner on the day in question, he appeared very drunk.
NOT GUILTY.
First Middlesex Jury, before W. Arabin, Esq.
[trialAccount t18210912-197]

1247. ANN SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of September, one handkerchief, value 2 s.; one waistcoat, value 4 s., and one pair of stockings, value 6 d., the goods of John Dixon.
JOHN DIXON. I live in George-street, St. Giles's. On Thursday, the 13th of September, about half-past eleven at night, I was at the Black Dog, George-street, and had these things in a bundle, I laid them down on the counter of the bar, the prisoner was there, while I was drinking some beer it was gone - I have not seen it since; I did not see her take it - there were three of us there, myself, my companion, and the prisoner - nobody else, except the landlady was there at the time. I saw her again on Saturday in Drury-lane, and charged her with stealing it. She said I could not take her up without an officer. I saw nobody else come into the house.
THOMAS EDWARDS. I am an officer. The prisoner was given in my charge, I searched her, but found nothing on her.
Prisoner's Defence. I drank with him and was in his company four hours, and went with him to several different houses, and went to a public-house where he was dancing - I came out and left him there.
JOHN DIXON. She did not drink with me and was not in my company.
NOT GUILTY.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18210912-199]
1248. CATHERINE SULLIVAN was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of August, 11 s., in monies numbered, the property of John Naish, from his person.
JOHN NAISH. I am out of place, and live in Charles-street, Westminster. On the 25th of August, about three o'clock in the morning, I was returning from Peckam, where I had been waiting. I was coming down Drury-lane with a friend, and met the prisoner, she went with me to Church-street - my friend had another female, we both went to the same house of ill fame. My friend was in the next room to us. I objected going with the prisoner, and offered her a shilling to go when the light came up, as she had two black eyes. I put eleven shillings in my handkerchief in my breeches pocket, and went to bed; and in three quarters of an hour I awoke, by hearing her calling to be let out. I jumped up and knocked against the wainscot to awake my friend - he came, and the prisoner came up stairs, as she could not get out. My friend fetched a watchman who came up; she strongly denied it, and took out 1 s. which I had given her, and a few halfpence; she declared it was all she had in the world, and wished her Maker might strike her dead if it was not - she stripped herself, the watchman put his hand behind her head, she screamed out, and he took the eleven shillings from among her hair, tied in a piece of a handkerchief.
LEWIS ROBERTS. I am a watchman. I was called to this house, No. 1, Church-street, St. Giles's. The man charged the prisoner with stealing his money - they were both in the room; she denied it, and said she had no money belonging to him. I at last found it in her hair, wrapped up in an old piece of handkerchief. I found a handkerchief on her which the corner matched.
Prisoner's Defence. He took me to this house, and refused to give me three shillings which he promised; andlocked me in. I went to the window to call the watchman. I concealed my own money in my hair. I never saw a farthing of his money.
LEWIS ROBERTS re-examined. She said two shillings of the eleven belonged to her, and the nine shillings belonged to the man, and that she took care of it for him.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Confined Six Months.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18210912-200]
1249. THOMAS MORLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of September, one shawl, value 12 s.; four yards of linen, value 4 s.; one remnant of cambric, value 3 s; three yards of muslin, value 2 s.; one yard of lawn, value 1 s.; one yard of cotton, value 1 s., and one pair of gloves, value 1 s., the goods of James Shoolbread and John Ferguson.
JAMES SHOOLBREAD. I am in partnership with John Ferguson, we are linen drapers and silk mercers, and live in Tottenham-court-road. I only know the property.
WILLIAM RIVERS NASH. I was servant to the prosecutors. On the 11th of September, the prisoner was porter to the house. I had suspicions and missed a shilling. I took him up stairs and told him, I suspected him. I left him in the room, and on returning, I found a duplicate partly torn on the ground, and part in his hand, but before I attempted to pick it up, he had torn it all. I took it off the ground, and by it I found the silk shawl at Wood's. I found the other property at the Grafton Arms, public-house, Grafton-street; he told me he had left a bundle there - I went and found it, and gave it to the head-borough.
WILLIAM REASCROFT. I am apprentice to Mr. Wood, a pawnbroker, who lives in High-street, Bloomsbury. The duplicate produced belongs to our shop, and is for a shawl pawned on the 10th of September, for 5 s., in the name of John Smith. I took it in, but do not recollect the person.
CHARLES COUSINS. I took the prisoner in charge on the 11th - the headborough gave me the bundle.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. They promised if I would confess and tell every thing, they would not hurt me, that they suspected somebody else more than me.
W. R. NASH. I suspected both the porters, and told him if he acknowledged where all the property was, I would do all I could with his master.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Confined Six Months and Whipped.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

1250. JOHN HURLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of August, one pocket-book, value 1 s., and one half-crown, the property of John Wilson, from his person.

[trialAccount t18210912-202]
1251. JEREMIAH SULLIVAN was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of September, one watch, value 10 s.; one coat, value 10 s.; one hat, value 5 s., and one pair of shoes, value 2 s., the goods of Neil Moran, from his person.
NEIL MORAN. I am an artificial-flower maker, and live in Greek-street, Soho. On Sunday night, the 9th of September, between twelve and one o'clock, I was locked out of my lodging, and wandered about the streets until five o'clock in the morning, I was quite sober. In George-street, St. Giles's, a man shoved me off the footway. I asked why he did it - he then knocked me down, and as I was getting up he knocked me down again; the prisoner then came up, and pretended to take my part, and another tall man with him - the man who knocked me down went away. I took the prisoner and the other man to a public-house, and treated them with half a gallon of beer for their civility; they told me they knew the man who had abused me so, and if I would go and lay down at their lodging and take some rest, they would get me some breakfast in the morning; they took me to No. 9, Church-street, into the parlour, there was a bed in the room and a woman, who I understood to be the wife of one of them; they told her to get breakfast ready by the time I got up; the prisoner helped me off with my coat as it was tight - I was quite sober. I took off my shoes and hat - I had my watch in my fob. It was about seven o'clock when I laid down, and about nine o'clock, I found the prisoner fumbling in my waistcoat pocket. I asked what was the matter, he said, "Nothing, I am only putting the clothes on you;" nobody but him was in the room. I got up and they were all off, and my coat, shoes, and hat also, and my watch was gone from my fob. I found the slider and ring of it on the bed - it was fastened to the ribbon - I had my watch with me when I left my lodging. I have found nothing since. I went out and alarmed the neighbourhood; the prisoner was taken between four and five o'clock that day. We took him to the public-house, and the landlord said he was one of the two who were with me, and he did not deny it - I am certain of him.
JAMES JORDAN. I took the prisoner in custody, he denied the charge. The prosecutor might have been drinking.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw this man and another stripped, and fighting, in George-street, the prosecutor was in liquor, and the other was striking him. I pulled him off - they fought for a quarter of an hour, then the prosecutor gave me some beer; he said he should like to lay down, and the man who drank with us said he could lay down at his place; four of us, and two girls of the town went with him, the man pulled off his coat and shoes. I went home in ten minutes, as I did not lodge there.
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Transported for Seven Years.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18210912-203]
1252. ELIZA STINTON was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of August, one gown, value 18 d.; one tippet, value 4 d., and two frocks, value 8 d., the goods of Eliza Byass.
ELIZA BYASS. I am a single woman, and live on Saffron-hill. On the 10th of August, these things hung up at the door. I deal in marine stores. About twelve o'clock they were taken.
SAMUEL DAVIS. I was coming out of Mr. Pontifex's of Shoe-lane, and saw a crowd round the prisoner, she had her arm and leg tied up, as if she was lame; two womencame up, and loosed her leg and arms, and one of them fought with her, and she ran off as hard as she could, I followed her, and was telling a person about her impositions, and turning round to point her out, I saw all these things under her arm. I knew the gown, and the prosecutrix claimed it.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. It is all false. I never saw them till it was brought to the office.
GUILTY. Aged 47.
Transported for Seven Years.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

1254. EMANUEL RUSSELL was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of August, one waistcoat, value 3 s., and one pair of stockings, value 1 s., the goods of Thomas Clarke.
ELIZA WOODS. The prisoner lodged with me seven or eight months. On the 29th of August, about twelve o'clock he came in and said he was rather the worse for liquor, and must go up stairs and lay down. He came down in about an hour, and went out with a bundle. I went up and missed these things from the cupboard, ran out but could not overtake him. I have never found the things.
NOT GUILTY.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18210912-206]
1255. ELIZA PURDIE was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of August, two frocks, value 2 s.; three napkins, value 1 s., and one bed-gown, value 9 d., the goods of Isaac Dowling.
ANN DOWLING. I am wife of Isaac Dowling, we live on Bowler's-green. These things were stolen while I laid in. The prisoner nurse d me. I had the linen from St. Andrew's Society, and during the month five napkins and a small frock were stolen, and many things of my own; she was a fortnight with me - I charged her with taking them; she said, she had carried them to the mangle - I mentioned my mangler's name, she said, it was not there, it was another person who she knew, and she would fetch them, she left, and I saw no more of her. A neighbour found them at the pawnbroker's; she sent me word that she would see me d - d before she would send me the duplicates.
Cross-examined by MR. HONE. Q. You do not know where she was - A. No. I have got the things to make good. Every thing was marked, I know she can read, and she had the same linen when she laid in herself.
ANN CROSSMAN. The prosecutrix is a neighbour of mine, she sent to me on missing the property; I met the prisoner by my door, she said, Mrs. Dowling had lost three napkins, that they were taken out of the yard, and asked me to go to Nicholls's with her, to ask if they had been pledged, I went, she said to Nicholls "Has any linen been pawned her belonging to St. Andrew's", he said, No. I went with her to Crees, another pawnbroker, and enquired there, and she went back to Dowling. I then went alone to Nicholls's; he said if any were pawned there, the prisoner was the woman who pawned them. I went to Armstrong's, and found some things pawned in the name of Glass, which I redeemed - they were wrapped in one of my aprons. A little girl fetched me into Leather-lane, where I saw the prisoner with her child, and asked why she took the things, she said she would make them good. I said "Why did you take them, you knew she was distressed, and has six children; give me the tickets and I will take them out." She said she would see her d - d first.
JOHN LIMBRIC. I was sent for and took her, and found the duplicates on her of things pawned at Mr. Nicholls's and some at Armstrong's, which he gave up, also a bed-gown and frock.
SOMERVILL TELFER. I am shopman to Mr. Nicholls, of Gray's Inn-lane. I have a frock, pin-cloth, two napkins, and a child's blanket. I was present when the prisoner pawned the pin-cloth and napkins in the name of Ann Glass. The duplicates found upon her are ours.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. She knew I pawned them.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Confined Six Months.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18210912-207]
1256. WILLIAM M'COY was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of September, one pair of trowsers, value 9 s., the goods of Joseph Harrison, privately in his shop.
EDWARD HULL. I am servant to Joseph Harrison, a pawnbroker, who lives in Tottenham-court-road. On the 10th of September, between eight and ten o'clock, these trowsers were stolen off the counter.
CHARLES PERRY. On the 10th of September, between ten and eleven o'clock in the morning, the prisoner came to my master's shop, (Mr. Hulm, of Museum-street,) and offered to pawn these trowsers for 7 s., my master said they were stolen; he said it was no such thing, that they were his father's who lived in Dudley-court - he persisted in saying so; we said we would send for the beadle, he said we might do so in welcome; he wanted to go with the boy for the beadle, but we kept him, he then said he lived in Lassells-court, he tried to get out, I held him, he beat and kicked me - an officer came and took him.
JOHN RICE. I took him in charge; he told me at the watch-house, that he stole them from Glass-house-yard, we went there, they missed nothing.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. A woman came up to me in Church-lane, and asked me to pawn them, and offered me sixpence. I said, "If they ask any questions, I will say they are my father's."
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Life.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18210912-208]
1257. ELEANOR MURPHY was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of September, one coat, value 5 s., the goods of George Whitfield Royer.
GEORGE WHITFIELD ROYER. I am a poulterer, and live in Great Marylebone-street. I was out when this happened.
JOSEPH PULLMAN. I am servant to Mr. Royer. On the14th of September, between two and three o'clock, I was in the kitchen, my mistress said there was some woman gone out with something. I ran out, the prisoner was pointed out to me going up Portland-place. I went up and asked if she had not been in the poulterer's shop, she said Yes; she had made a mistake, and took the wrong coat, and was bringing it back, that she had left her own there for 1 s., then she said, she went in and saw this coat, and thinking it was hers, she took it. I said she took it without seeing any body; she said she saw the good lady. She had left no coat in our shop - I do not know her. It was taken from the parlour - it could not be seen without coming into the parlour behind the shop.
HENRY BUCKERIDGE. I am an officer. I was sent for, and took her in charge; she said she left her coat at a poulterer's for sixpence, and could not tell the shop. I went to several poulterer's, nobody knew her.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I bought sixpenny-worth of giblets, and left my coat. I went into the shop, nobody was there but a lady reading. I took the coat and said, "Thank you;" it rained, and I put it over my head, found it was not mine, and was coming back with it.
GUILTY.
Confined Six Months.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18210912-209]
1258. SAMUEL MIDGLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of September, one bedstead, value 10 s.; the frame of a four-post bedstead, value 10 s.; one leather bag, value 3 s., and one sack, value 2 s., the goods of George Mitchell.
GEORGE MITCHELL. I am an upholsterer, and live at Tottenham. On the 10th of September, between seven and eight o'clock in the morning, I missed these things from my back warehouse. When I came to town, the officer informed me that the prisoner was in custody - he is a shoemaker at Tottenham.
WILLIAM TURNNARD. I am an officer. About ten o'clock on the night of the 10th of September, I went to take the prisoner - I believe he was in bed, he came down. I told him I took him for stealing some property from Mitchell; he acknowledged taking it, and took me to an out-house adjoining his own house, where he had concealed it. Mr. Mitchell claimed it - he fell on his knees and begged him not to prosecute him.
HENRY MITCHELL. I employed the prisoner to clear the warehouse out, and sent him out with a four-post bedstead; a few days after, I heard he had robbed my father.
THOMAS BENSON. I am a gardener, at Tottenham. I saw the prisoner take these things out of the place, and put them in a hay-cart; he said "Mr. Mitchell employed me to take them." It appeared two bedsteads tied up.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. The witness, Henry Mitchell, employed me to fetch his cart from the wheelers, after that he set me to clean the horse and cart to go to Barnet races, and to keep it a secret from his father; he said he would give me something handsome if I attended him on his return. He told me to keep the horse and cart out of the way till his father was gone; he then set me at different jobs, and asked me to buy one bedstead and to take another to another person, and told me to put them in the hay cart, and if his father met me I was to say I was going to take them to Murray's; he was in the habit of selling things unknown to his parents; he said they would not miss these; he used to pay people in furniture for work they did, unknown to his father.
HENRY MITCHELL. It is not true.
GUILTY. Aged 42.
Transported for Seven Years.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

1262. GEORGE HARRISON was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of July, one watch, value 50 s.; two seals,value 30 s.; one chain, value 1 s., and one key, value 6 d., the goods of John Hazell.
JOHN HAZELL. I am an accountant, and lodge at the prisoner's mother's, in Well-street, Oxford-street. On Sunday morning, the 23d of July, I left my apartments to go to Epping-forest. I left the key of the room with his mother. I returned next day between eight and nine at night, and my watch was gone from the case on the mantle-piece. I suspected him. Jones took him three days after, in bed. He lived with his mother, who is a widow.
JOHN JONES. The prisoner's uncle came to me, and told me I should find him in bed at a public-house, in Clipstone-street, Marylebone. I was up there all night, and at five o'clock in the morning, a man said he was in bed at home. I went up three pair of stairs, and saw him in bed, and I told him I wanted him about a watch, he denied knowing any thing of it. I locked him up at the watch-house; his uncle wished to speak to him. I brought him out, and he said he sold it for 13 s., at No. 13, Crown-street, Soho; for three half crowns, 6 d., and 5 s. He took me to the shop, kept by Carey, it is a rag-shop. I did not find it. He told me as he went along that he had taken his cousin's shawl which cost two guineas and a half, and sold it at the same shop for 5 s. He belongs to very respectable parents, but has had connexions.
Prisoner's Defence. I know nothing whatever of it.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

1263. GEORGE GRUDGINGTON, JOHN BOSWELL, and CHARLES SEAWARD, were indicted for stealing, on the 1st of September, one milk pot, value 1 s., the goods Timothy Greated.
TIMOTHY GREATED. I am clerk to the London Insurance Company. I know nothing of the transaction.
GEORGE TAYLOR. On the 1st of September, about eight o'clock in the morning, I saw all the prisoners in company together, coming down Pentonville. Boswell had an apron tied before him, he went up two garden walks, came out and joined the other two. He then went up the walk of No. 18, Pleasant-row, and got in at the window which was open, and came out with the milk-pot in his hand, and was stuffing it in among some shavings which he he had in his apron; the other two waited for him two doors off - he joined them. I took him and pulled the pot out, and asked where he got it, he made no answer. I took him to the house, the servant claimed it. I kept all three in custody.
JOSEPH CADBY. I saw the prisoners coming down, I secured Grudgington and Seaward, they made great resistance.
JOHN HUTT. I assisted Cadby in taking them. I had seen them all three together the morning before.
GRUDGINGTON - GUILTY. Aged 19.
BOSWELL. - GUILTY. Aged 19.
SEAWARD. - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Confined Six Months, and Whipped.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.

1266. CAROLINE GOODWYN was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of August, one cloak, value 2 s.; one petticoat, value 1 s.; and one gown body, value 6 d., the goods of David Airey.
DOROTHY AIREY. I am wife of David Airey, who is a carpenter. The prisoner was stopping at our house till she could get a situation. A young woman gave me information - I missed these things out of my chest and suspected her. They were at a gentleman's house which I had the care of - the family had given her leave to be there.
MARIA HAMMON. The prisoner came to tea with me and gave me a white cloak - she said it was one of her little girls. Airey claimed it.
MARY JUDGE. The prisoner gave me a white gown body, and said it was an old one of her own - I gave it to Airey.
WILLIAM WESTCOAT. On the 10th of September, Mrs. Airey gave me information - I went to North-end, Fulham, to Mr. Kent's, where the prisoner was in service - searched her trunks and found a petticoat and part of a gold seal.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. On the Thursday before the robbery, I was next door and returned about eight o'clock, the street door was open, and I lost some shifts, stockings, and four sovereigns. Another box was robbed of several things, and a counterpane of my master's - they said next day they had lost a purse, containing twenty guineas, and hoped I would keep it secret, or they would be discharged, I agreed. As to the things, I do not know how I became possessed of them, unless they got mixed with mine at the mangle. The prosecutrix was much embittered against me for not conniving at her changing some feathers out of the bed - she said she would be my destruction, unless I gave her a sum of money.
DOROTHY AIREY. I never had a dispute with her.
GUILTY. Aged 32.
Transported for Seven Years.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18210912-218]

JOHN M'PHERSON. I keep a public-house in Globe-lane, Mile-end. The prisoner was an occasional customer. On the 26th of August, he came for a pipe of tobacco, I looked through a crevice in the kitchen door and heard the lock of the till go. I ran out, pulled him over the counter, with half-a-crown and sixpence in his hand; he said it was the first time he served me so; I said I thought he had robbed me several times - he acknowledged robbing me six times.
Prisoner's Defence. I pulled out 3 s. to see what money I had, the gentleman came and said, "You rascal, you have been robbing the till."

1270. GEORGE ATKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, one watch, value 14 s.; one seal, value 1 s., and two keys, value 1 d., the property of Anthony Carvile, from his person.
ANTHONY CARVILE. I am a seaman. The prisoner came out of the London-docks, with a pipe in his hand - he passed me, came back, threw his pipe down, pushed me, and took my watch out of my pocket - he got away with it.
JOSEPH KING. I was in the Red Lion, public-house, Nightingale-lane, I saw the prisoner there, he followed me and the prosecutor out, and by the London-docks he turned back; I saw him push the prosecutor down and snatch his watch - I followed and secured him, and with assistance, took the watch from him. Two or three came up and said, "Knock him down." I was obliged to let him go - he was taken next day. I am sure he is the man, he was quite sober.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOHN CLIFTON. I am a patrol. About half-past ten o'clock, I was informed of the robbery, and had a description of the man, I took him in half-an-hour; he said the man had got his watch again and it was nothing to me - he was sober.
Prisoner's Defence. I was very much in liquor. The man said at the office that somebody else took it.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Seven Years.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18210912-222]
1271. JAMES BULL was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of May, four sacks, value 10 s., and sixteen bushels of beans, value 3 l., the goods of Isaac Ennos.
ISAAC ENNOS. I am a miller and corn-dealer, and live at Wilsden. On the 19th of May, 1820, a man who I believe to be the prisoner (but had rather not swear to him) came and asked how I sold beans, I said, 34 s. a quarter - he offered 33 s., and told me to send them to-morrow, or it would not do - I sent my man with them to No. 98, Chapel-street, Marylebone - I did not see the prisoner afterwards, he was to send the money back by the man, on delivery - I did no mean to part with them without the money; I gave the carter a receipt - he said he lived at No. 40, there is no such number. He bought them for himself, and said his name was Wilson, Chapel-street.
JOHN BARNES. I am carman to Mr. Ennos. The prisoner met me at the corner of Bell-street, and asked if I had any corn for him, I said, I had some for somebody, and shewed him the bill - he said, "It is for me, we will take them to the shoemaker's shop below, and pitch one quarter at the door, and take the other up stairs" - I pitched both quarters at the door; he asked if I had any bran, I said No; he said he would get the money and sacks ready by when I returned - I went down the wharf, returned and he was gone, sacks and all - I did not intend to leave them without the money - I am sure he is the man.
Prisoner. Q. When I met you, did you not say they were for me - A. No; I shewed you the bill, you said, "That is right."
ANN DAVIS. The man brought the beans and pitched them at my door, and said he would fetch the cart and horse to take them away; he brought a strange cart and horse and took them away - he did not live at my house - he came three weeks before to take a room which I had to let; another man took them away.
JOHN TUNKS. I carted the beans from Davis's house to Tottenham-court-road for the prisoner, he is the man; he never paid me, but said he had another job - I cannot positively swear that he is the man.
JOHN LIMBRIC. I had information of this robbery, twelve months ago; I have been looking after the prisoner ever since.
WILLIAM MASON. I live at Camden-town. About eighteen months ago, I bought two quarters of beans of the prisoner, not knowing whose they where, he brought them between ten and eleven o'clock in the day, I took them to Salisbury's, a neighbour, weighed them, and gave him 2 l. 6 s., and a pot of porter for them; he took the sacks back - the market price was 2 l. 8 s.
ISAAC ENNOS. They were worth 3 l. 18 s.
- HANSON. I live in Henry-street, Hampstead. A man asked if I wanted a job, he took me to Palace-row, where there where four sacks of beans. I took themto Camden-town - Mason told me to carry them to be weighed; Mason said to me "Come on" - I shot some of the corn at his house, he sent me over the way to get a pot of beer, and the man who employed me gave me 1 s. 6 d. I cannot say that the prisoner is the man, he may be, I left them at Mason's, sacks, corn, and all.
Prisoner's Defence. On the 12th of April, I went to a lime wharf at Paddington with my cart, stopped at the Red Lion, public-house, Edgware-road, and met a man who called himself Mr. Wilson, a corn-dealer, of Edgware-road. He said he wished to exchange his cart away for a good horse; my horse came to 7 l. 10 s., and his cart to 4 l. 10 s. He proposed to pay the 3 l. in about a week, I refused; he said he would furnish me with two quarters of beans, at 30 s. a quarter - I agreed, and was to meet him next morning, and on the way, I met him; he said he was going to Tottenham-court-road, and expected the beans would be there soon, and authorized me to take them in my possession. I went to the White Lion, public-house, saw the cart, and asked the carter if the beans were for me, he said, "Yes." I asked if he had brought any bran, he said, "No;" I received the beans, and left them at my house.
GUILTY. Aged 34.
Transported for Seven Years.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[supplementaryMaterial o18210912-1]
WILLIAM HAYNES and WILLIAM HARRISON, convicted of Burglary at the last February Sessions, (Vide page 128), but whose judgment was respited; received Sentence of Death, but no public decision has been delivered.
